That’s really what made me come in.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed needing less sleep lately, or finding it easier to function with less rest than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… at least not in the way you’re asking. I mean, I’m not walking around thinking, “Oh, great, I only slept a few hours and I feel amazing.” It’s more that I’m busy and wound up, and then sleep becomes one more thing I’m not very settled about.\n\nBut needing less sleep—like actually functioning better or feeling unusually rested on less—I haven’t really noticed that. If I don’t get enough sleep, I still feel it. I might keep going because there are things to do, but I wouldn’t say it feels easy or like I suddenly don’t need rest.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling unusually happy, elated, or euphoric lately, even when things aren’t particularly going well?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say that. I mean, I have moments where I’m pleased if something goes well, like if a student has a good day or I manage to get through something I’ve been putting off, but that feels normal to me. I haven’t felt unusually happy or euphoric out of nowhere, especially not when things are actually stressful or not going well.\n\nIf anything, when things aren’t going well, I’m aware they’re not going well. I don’t suddenly feel on top of the world or anything like that. I’m more just… trying to keep moving through it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself becoming more impatient or irritable, especially when you’re trying to get things done or when something slows you down?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d describe it that way, exactly. I mean… I can get frustrated when something slows me down, sure, but I don’t know if it’s more than what anyone would feel when they’re trying to get through a long day and there are a hundred interruptions.\n\nAt school, there are so many moving parts that it’s hard to tell what’s “irritable” versus just… reacting to the situation, if that makes sense. Like, if I’m trying to get something done and I’m interrupted, I might feel that jolt of annoyance, but I don’t think I’ve been walking around snapping at people or feeling unusually angry. At least, I haven’t noticed that as the main problem.\n\nIt’s more that I feel driven to keep going, and when something blocks me, I notice the pressure of it. But impatience or irritability specifically… I’d have to think about that. It hasn’t stood out to me the way the restlessness has.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your thoughts feeling especially fast or racing lately, like one idea quickly jumps to the next before you can fully process anything?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it racing, exactly. I mean, my mind is often busy, but when you say “racing,” I picture something kind of extreme, like thoughts coming so fast I can’t keep up with them at all, or like I’m being carried along by them. I don’t think I’ve noticed that in a clear way.\n\nSometimes I’ll move from one thing to another in my head because there’s a lot to keep track of, and I can get kind of mentally crowded, if that makes sense. But I’m still aware of what I’m thinking about. It’s not like my thoughts are suddenly speeding ahead in some unusual way or like one idea is exploding into the next before I can process anything.\n\nSo… no, not especially fast or racing lately. At least not that I’ve recognized as something different from my usual busy brain.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to focus or stay on task lately, like your attention is easily pulled away by things around you or by your own thoughts?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly, because I do feel like my attention gets pulled around, but I don’t know if it’s “lately” in the sense of something new or noticeably worse. It’s more like… when I’m trying to stay with one thing, especially if there are noises or people moving around or I remember something else I’m supposed to do, my attention can sort of jump over there before I’ve finished what I was doing.\n\nBut I wouldn’t want to overstate it as if I’ve suddenly become unable to focus out of nowhere. It’s not like I sit down and every single time I can’t do anything. I can focus sometimes, especially if there’s something right in front of me that really demands it. But yes, I guess I do notice that staying on one task takes effort, and if something interrupts me, it can take a while to get back to where I was.\n\nSo… harder to focus, maybe, but I’m not totally sure whether I’d call it a recent change or just something I’m more aware of when things are busy.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more quickly or feeling physically restless, like you can't sit still or slow down your body even if you want to?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s probably the clearest way to describe what’s been bothering me, actually. I feel like I’m constantly running on a motor lately. Like at school, I finish one thing and before I’ve even really had a second to register that it’s done, I’m already moving into the next thing—physically moving, mentally moving, just… going.\n\nAnd it’s the same at home. I’ll get through one task and then immediately jump into something else, and it doesn’t feel like a calm choice, exactly. It feels more like I can’t shut myself off. Even if part of me thinks, “Okay, sit down for a minute,” my body is already up and doing the next thing. I don’t feel settled. It’s like there’s this engine in me that keeps pushing, and slowing down feels almost impossible, even when I know I should.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any period lasting at least two weeks where you felt down most of the time, lost interest in things you usually enjoy, or experienced other symptoms like changes in appetite, trouble sleeping, or feeling hopeless?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure I can point to a period like that, at least not clearly. I mean, I’ve had times where I’m worn out or frustrated, and I can get discouraged when I feel like I’m not managing things the way I should, but when you say “two weeks” and “down most of the time,” that sounds more specific than what I can confidently say I’ve had.\n\nI don’t think I’ve had a stretch where I just lost interest in everything I normally enjoy, or felt hopeless most of the day, every day. At least, nothing is jumping out to me like, “Yes, that was definitely that.” My mood can shift with how the day is going, and if I’m overwhelmed I can feel pretty low in the moment, but it doesn’t feel like this steady, heavy thing lasting weeks.\n\nAs far as appetite or sleep, I’d have to think about it more, because those can vary for normal reasons too, but I haven’t noticed a big pattern that I’d connect to feeling depressed. So… I guess my answer is no, not that I’ve recognized.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting at least four days where you felt unusually high, overly energetic, or unusually irritable, along with a noticeable increase in your activity level or restlessness, more than what you'd normally experience during a busy day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve had something like that in the way you’re describing it. I mean, I do have times where I’m very keyed up and restless, and other people might probably see me as moving too fast or being hard to slow down, but when you say a period of at least four days where I felt unusually high or noticeably different from myself, I’m not sure I can separate it out like that.\n\nI don’t really think of it as feeling “high,” exactly. Overly energetic, maybe at times, but it feels more like being unable to settle than like I’m in some amazing mood or anything. And irritable… I can get irritable if I feel interrupted or if there’s too much going on, but I don’t know that I’ve had a clear several-day stretch where my mood was unusually elevated or irritable in a way that stood out as a separate episode.\n\nSo I guess my answer is: not that I’ve recognized. I can describe the restlessness, but I don’t know that I’ve had that four-day kind of period you’re asking about.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently, such as a significant decrease in how much you eat or unintentional weight loss?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. At least, I haven’t noticed anything dramatic like that. My appetite seems more or less the same to me, and my weight hasn’t changed in a way that I’m aware of.\n\nI mean, there are days where I might eat at odd times just because the day gets busy or I’m moving from one thing to the next, but I wouldn’t call it a significant decrease, and I haven’t had people saying I look like I’ve lost weight or anything like that. So, no, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you take less pleasure or interest in activities you usually enjoy, even things that normally make you feel good?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a broad way. I still enjoy the things I usually enjoy when I actually sit down and do them, if that makes sense. Like, I don’t feel like everything has gone flat or that I can’t get pleasure from things anymore.\n\nSometimes I might have trouble getting myself started on something, or I might jump from one thing to another and not stay with it long enough, but that feels different from not enjoying it. Once I’m engaged, I can still get into it and feel good about it.\n\nSo, no, I haven’t really noticed a loss of interest or pleasure like you’re describing. Not as a consistent change, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt noticeably more energetic or restless for several days in a row, even if your mood wasn’t necessarily better than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes, that part I do recognize, definitely. I don’t know that I’d describe it as my mood being better or anything like that, but the restlessness and energy, yes. Lately it feels like I’m constantly running on a motor.\n\nAt school, I’ll finish one thing and instead of pausing or just taking a breath, I’m immediately onto the next thing—checking something, organizing something, going to talk to someone, fixing something in the classroom, whatever it is. And then I get home and it’s kind of the same pattern. I’ll do one task and then jump right into another, like I can’t shut myself off.\n\nIt’s not really a “good mood” feeling. It’s more like I’m being pushed from the inside, if that makes sense. Like there’s always another thing I have to move toward, and sitting still or being done for the day feels almost uncomfortable. So yes, several days in a row, that happens. I mean, I’ve had stretches where I’m restless or wound up, like I said, and I can be hard to settle, but the mood part you’re asking about — unusually elevated or expansive most of the day, nearly every day for a week — I don’t really recognize that in myself.\n\nI don’t think I’ve had a week where I felt, you know, unusually great or bigger than myself or on top of the world in that way. And with irritability, I mean, I can get irritated like anyone can, especially if things are chaotic or I’m being interrupted, but I don’t know that it’s been this clear period of a week where my mood was abnormally irritable all day, every day.\n\nSo I’d say no, not that I’ve noticed. I’m not sure I’d say yes to that. I mean, when you list all of those together, I can think of individual things here and there at different times, but I don’t know that I’ve had a solid two-week stretch where five or more of them were happening nearly every day.

The “difficulty concentrating” part is tricky for me, because that can be hard to separate out from how I am generally, so I don’t want to over-answer that and make it sound like it suddenly came on during a depressed period. And I’ve had tired days or nights where sleep wasn’t great, but again, I don’t know that it was part of a two-week period with depressed mood and all those other symptoms stacked together.

So I guess my answer would be no, not that I’ve clearly noticed. I don’t remember a two-week period where I had five or more of those symptoms in the way you’re describing.

And with irritability, I mean, I can get irritated like anyone can, especially if things are chaotic or I’m being interrupted, but I don’t know that it’s been this clear period of a week where my mood was abnormally irritable all day, every day.\n\nSo I’d say no, not that I’ve noticed. The energy and restlessness are more familiar to me than a distinct mood change lasting that long.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting at least four consecutive days where your mood was persistently elevated, unusually irritable, or both, most of the day, nearly every day, not just in response to something happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’m trying to be careful because four days is shorter than a week, so I don’t want to just automatically say no without thinking about it. But when you put it that way — mood being persistently elevated or unusually irritable most of the day, nearly every day, for four days straight — I don’t really recognize a clear period like that.\n\nI’ve had days where I’m more keyed up or where I’m moving around a lot and can’t seem to settle, and I’ve had days where I’m more easily annoyed, sure. But usually that feels tied to what’s going on around me, or it comes and goes during the day. It’s not like I wake up for four days in a row and I’m in this noticeably different mood state the whole time.\n\nAnd I definitely wouldn’t say I’ve had four days of feeling unusually elevated or expansive, like everything is amazing or I’m somehow different from my usual self. That doesn’t sound familiar. If anything, the thing I notice is more physical or behavioral — being busy, restless, hard to slow down — but not a sustained mood change like you’re describing. I mean, I’ve had low moments, of course, and days where I feel worn down or discouraged, especially when things pile up, but when you say two weeks, most of the day, nearly every day, and not just because something happened — I don’t think that really fits.\n\nI don’t remember having a stretch where I woke up day after day for two weeks feeling down or hopeless in that steady, all-day kind of way. I don’t think I can say that I have, at least not in the way you’re laying it out. I know you asked something similar a minute ago, and I’m trying not to just give you a quick automatic “no,” because I do want to answer it accurately.

When I look back, I can think of times where maybe one or two things were going on — like maybe I was tired, or my sleep wasn’t great, or I was having trouble focusing — but those don’t really stand out to me as part of a two-week stretch where I also felt down most of the day and lost interest in things and had several of those other symptoms all together.

The concentration piece is always hard for me to judge, because it’s not like I can clearly say, “Oh, for those two weeks I suddenly couldn’t concentrate because I was depressed.” That’s just not how I remember it. And I don’t really recall a period where I was barely eating, sleeping way too much or not sleeping, feeling worthless, and having no energy all clustered together for two weeks.

So, no — not that I’ve noticed. I don’t remember a two-week period with five or more of those happening in that pattern.

At least not that I’ve recognized as something different from my usual busy brain.\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to focus or stay on task lately, like your attention is easily pulled away by things around you or by your own thoughts?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly, because I do feel like my attention gets pulled around, but I don’t know if it’s “lately” in the sense of something new or noticeably worse. It’s more like… when I’m trying to stay with one thing, especially if there are noises or people moving around or I remember something else I’m supposed to do, my attention can sort of jump over there before I’ve finished what I was doing.\n\nBut I wouldn’t want to overstate it as if I’ve suddenly become unable to focus out of nowhere. But yes, I guess I do notice that staying on one task takes effort, and if something interrupts me, it can take a while to get back to where I was.\n\nSo… harder to focus, maybe, but I’m not totally sure whether I’d call it a recent change or just something I’m more aware of when things are busy.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more quickly or feeling physically restless, like you can't sit still or slow down your body even if you want to?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s probably the clearest way to describe what’s been bothering me, actually. I feel like I’m constantly running on a motor lately. Like at school, I finish one thing and before I’ve even really had a second to register that it’s done, I’m already moving into the next thing—physically moving, mentally moving, just… going.\n\nAnd it’s the same at home. I mean, I’ve had times where I’m worn out or frustrated, and I can get discouraged when I feel like I’m not managing things the way I should, but when you say “two weeks” and “down most of the time,” that sounds more specific than what I can confidently say I’ve had.\n\nI don’t think I’ve had a stretch where I just lost interest in everything I normally enjoy, or felt hopeless most of the day, every day. At least, nothing is jumping out to me like, “Yes, that was definitely that.” My mood can shift with how the day is going, and if I’m overwhelmed I can feel pretty low in the moment, but it doesn’t feel like this steady, heavy thing lasting weeks.\n\nAs far as appetite or sleep, I’d have to think about it more, because those can vary for normal reasons too, but I haven’t noticed a big pattern that I’d connect to feeling depressed. So… I guess my answer is no, not that I’ve recognized.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting at least four days where you felt unusually high, overly energetic, or unusually irritable, along with a noticeable increase in your activity level or restlessness, more than what you'd normally experience during a busy day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve had something like that in the way you’re describing it. I mean, I do have times where I’m very keyed up and restless, and other people might probably see me as moving too fast or being hard to slow down, but when you say a period of at least four days where I felt unusually high or noticeably different from myself, I’m not sure I can separate it out like that.\n\nI don’t really think of it as feeling “high,” exactly. Overly energetic, maybe at times, but it feels more like being unable to settle than like I’m in some amazing mood or anything. And irritable… I can get irritable if I feel interrupted or if there’s too much going on, but I don’t know that I’ve had a clear several-day stretch where my mood was unusually elevated or irritable in a way that stood out as a separate episode.\n\nSo I guess my answer is: not that I’ve recognized. I can describe the restlessness, but I don’t know that I’ve had that four-day kind of period you’re asking about.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently, such as a significant decrease in how much you eat or unintentional weight loss?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. At least, I haven’t noticed anything dramatic like that. My appetite seems more or less the same to me, and my weight hasn’t changed in a way that I’m aware of.\n\nI mean, there are days where I might eat at odd times just because the day gets busy or I’m moving from one thing to the next, but I wouldn’t call it a significant decrease, and I haven’t had people saying I look like I’ve lost weight or anything like that. So, no, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you take less pleasure or interest in activities you usually enjoy, even things that normally make you feel good?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a broad way. I still enjoy the things I usually enjoy when I actually sit down and do them, if that makes sense. Like, I don’t feel like everything has gone flat or that I can’t get pleasure from things anymore.\n\nSometimes I might have trouble getting myself started on something, or I might jump from one thing to another and not stay with it long enough, but that feels different from not enjoying it. Once I’m engaged, I can still get into it and feel good about it.\n\nSo, no, I haven’t really noticed a loss of interest or pleasure like you’re describing. Not as a consistent change, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt noticeably more energetic or restless for several days in a row, even if your mood wasn’t necessarily better than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes, that part I do recognize, definitely. I don’t know that I’d describe it as my mood being better or anything like that, but the restlessness and energy, yes. Lately it feels like I’m constantly running on a motor.\n\nAt school, I’ll finish one thing and instead of pausing or just taking a breath, I’m immediately onto the next thing—checking something, organizing something, going to talk to someone, fixing something in the classroom, whatever it is. And then I get home and it’s kind of the same pattern. I’ll do one task and then jump right into another, like I can’t shut myself off.\n\nIt’s not really a “good mood” feeling. It’s more like I’m being pushed from the inside, if that makes sense. Like there’s always another thing I have to move toward, and sitting still or being done for the day feels almost uncomfortable. So yes, several days in a row, that happens. It’s more constant activity than feeling unusually happy.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting at least a week where your mood was unusually elevated, expansive, or irritable most of the day, nearly every day, not just during brief moments or in response to things going well?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that, no. I mean, I’ve had stretches where I’m restless or wound up, like I said, and I can be hard to settle, but the mood part you’re asking about — unusually elevated or expansive most of the day, nearly every day for a week — I don’t really recognize that in myself.\n\nI don’t think I’ve had a week where I felt, you know, unusually great or bigger than myself or on top of the world in that way. And with irritability, I mean, I can get irritated like anyone can, especially if things are chaotic or I’m being interrupted, but I don’t know that it’s been this clear period of a week where my mood was abnormally irritable all day, every day.\n\nSo I’d say no, not that I’ve noticed. The energy and restlessness are more familiar to me than a distinct mood change lasting that long.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting at least four consecutive days where your mood was persistently elevated, unusually irritable, or both, most of the day, nearly every day, not just in response to something happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’m trying to be careful because four days is shorter than a week, so I don’t want to just automatically say no without thinking about it. But when you put it that way — mood being persistently elevated or unusually irritable most of the day, nearly every day, for four days straight — I don’t really recognize a clear period like that.\n\nI’ve had days where I’m more keyed up or where I’m moving around a lot and can’t seem to settle, and I’ve had days where I’m more easily annoyed, sure. But usually that feels tied to what’s going on around me, or it comes and goes during the day. It’s not like I wake up for four days in a row and I’m in this noticeably different mood state the whole time.\n\nAnd I definitely wouldn’t say I’ve had four days of feeling unusually elevated or expansive, like everything is amazing or I’m somehow different from my usual self. That doesn’t sound familiar. If anything, the thing I notice is more physical or behavioral — being busy, restless, hard to slow down — but not a sustained mood change like you’re describing. So I’d say no, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks where you felt down or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, not just in response to something happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can point to a period like that, no. I mean, I’ve had low moments, of course, and days where I feel worn down or discouraged, especially when things pile up, but when you say two weeks, most of the day, nearly every day, and not just because something happened — I don’t think that really fits.\n\nI don’t remember having a stretch where I woke up day after day for two weeks feeling down or hopeless in that steady, all-day kind of way. If I get upset or discouraged, it usually feels connected to whatever is going on at the time, and then it shifts. It doesn’t feel like this heavy mood that stays there nearly every day for weeks.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think I’ve had that. At least not that I’ve noticed or would describe that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you need significantly less sleep than usual, like feeling rested after only a few hours of sleep when you would normally need much more?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had nights where I don’t sleep well, or where I’m up later than I meant to be, but I don’t wake up after, say, three or four hours feeling refreshed and like I’m good to go. If anything, when I sleep less than usual, I feel it.\n\nI’m trying to think if there’s ever been a stretch where I needed way less sleep and still felt rested, because I know that’s a different kind of thing than just being busy or staying up too late. But no, that doesn’t really sound like me. I still seem to need my usual amount of sleep, and if I don’t get it, I’m not magically energized or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period lasting at least two weeks where you experienced five or more of the following: depressed mood most of the day, markedly diminished interest in activities, significant weight loss or decrease in appetite, insomnia or hypersomnia nearly every day, psychomotor agitation or retardation, fatigue or loss of energy, feelings of worthlessness or guilt, or difficulty concentrating?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say yes to that. I mean, when you list all of those together, I can think of individual things here and there at different times, but I don’t know that I’ve had a solid two-week stretch where five or more of them were happening nearly every day.\n\nThe “difficulty concentrating” part is tricky for me, because that can be hard to separate out from how I am generally, so I don’t want to over-answer that and make it sound like it suddenly came on during a depressed period. And I’ve had tired days or nights where sleep wasn’t great, but again, I don’t know that it was part of a two-week period with depressed mood and all those other symptoms stacked together.\n\nSo I guess my answer would be no, not that I’ve clearly noticed. I don’t remember a two-week period where I had five or more of those symptoms in the way you’re describing.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, have you had a two-week period where you experienced five or more of the following: feeling down most of the day, losing interest in things you usually enjoy, eating much less than usual, having trouble sleeping or sleeping too much, feeling physically restless or slowed down, having no energy, feeling guilty or worthless, or having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say that I have, at least not in the way you’re laying it out. I know you asked something similar a minute ago, and I’m trying not to just give you a quick automatic “no,” because I do want to answer it accurately.\n\nWhen I look back, I can think of times where maybe one or two things were going on — like maybe I was tired, or my sleep wasn’t great, or I was having trouble focusing — but those don’t really stand out to me as part of a two-week stretch where I also felt down most of the day and lost interest in things and had several of those other symptoms all together.\n\nThe concentration piece is always hard for me to judge, because it’s not like I can clearly say, “Oh, for those two weeks I suddenly couldn’t concentrate because I was depressed.” That’s just not how I remember it. And I don’t really recall a period where I was barely eating, sleeping way too much or not sleeping, feeling worthless, and having no energy all clustered together for two weeks.\n\nSo, no — not that I’ve noticed. I don’t remember a two-week period with five or more of those happening in that pattern.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It’s not like I sit down and every single time I can’t do anything. I can focus sometimes, especially if there’s something right in front of me that really demands it. But yes, I guess I do notice that staying on one task takes effort, and if something interrupts me, it can take a while to get back to where I was.\n\nSo… harder to focus, maybe, but I’m not totally sure whether I’d call it a recent change or just something I’m more aware of when things are busy.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more quickly or feeling physically restless, like you can't sit still or slow down your body even if you want to?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s probably the clearest way to describe what’s been bothering me, actually. I feel like I’m constantly running on a motor lately. I mean, I’ve had stretches where I’m restless or wound up, like I said, and I can be hard to settle, but the mood part you’re asking about — unusually elevated or expansive most of the day, nearly every day for a week — I don’t really recognize that in myself.\n\nI don’t think I’ve had a week where I felt, you know, unusually great or bigger than myself or on top of the world in that way. But usually that feels tied to what’s going on around me, or it comes and goes during the day.
